{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VqyNoAvXU5Jk3qz7Tbv2tJ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Repository guidance\n2\t\n3\t## Product intent\n4\t\n5\tThis repository is an agent-native CRM framework. Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n\n## Medusa-inspired mapping\n\n| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n|---|---|\n| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n| Workflow | Commercial process |\n| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n\n## Layers\n\n```text\nAdmin / API / SDK\n        ↓\nWorkflows and policies\n        ↓\nCRM module services\n        ↓\nSQLite adapter\n\nMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\nTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n```\n\n## Modules\n\nA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n\nCurrent modules:\n\n- Company\n- Contact\n- Opportunity\n- Approval\n\n## Workflows\n\nA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n\nCurrent workflows:\n\n- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n\n## Actions\n\nA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n\nThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n\nUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n\n## Domain packages\n\nDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n\n`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n\n## Providers\n\nProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n\n## MCP responsibilities\n\nMCP exposes:\n\n- project and CRM context as resources;\n- controlled application operations as tools;\n- reusable build/debug prompts.\n\nIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n\n## Extension rule\n\nA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n\n1. one module;\n2. one workflow;\n3. one API or MCP surface;\n4. tests;\n5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n","numLines":92,"startLine":1,"totalLines":92}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WT6BDLsmUUBCnsqU32pukh","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Scenario evidence\n2\t\n3\t`accordo scenario run <scenario> [--json] [--root dir]` — **DX6**.\n4\t\n5\t> **Which JTBD rows does this checkout actually earn — and which does it not?**\n6\t\n7\tJob status in this repository lives in `docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` and\n8\tits generated index `docs/benchmarks/jobs.json`. Both are maintained by people.\n9\tNothing recomputed them, and nothing could tell you what a given run did **not**\n10\testablish — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` lists that as a known limitation, and AX1\n11\tsays it in its own report: `evidence.status` is `not_aggregated`, with a note\n12\tthat the JTBD documents are referenced by path and never parsed.\n13\t\n14\tThis command closes that gap, and only that gap.\n15\t\n16\t```text\n17\taccordo app inspect      what is composed?                             source facts\n18\taccordo solution check   is this plan still compatible?                a document\n19\taccordo project doctor   what is stale in the source?                  cheap\n20\taccordo project verify   can we PROVE the project is healthy?          expensive\n21\taccordo scenario run     which business jobs does this checkout earn?  this\n22\taccordo solution verify  is the plan actually finished?                DX10, which runs this\n23\t```\n24\t\n25\t## Two consumers, and what the second one changed\n26\t\n27\tA generic contract validated by exactly one consumer is not generic. It is a\n28\tshape fitted to that consumer, wearing a version number.\n29\t\n30\t`scenarioRunContract: 1` shipped with one scenario — `lead-to-won`, a sales\n31\tfunnel. The second one, `service-sla-escalation`, was written specifically to be\n32\t*unlike* it: Service is package-native, has a declared state machine, real clock\n33\tsemantics and immutable evidence records, and its load-bearing outcomes are\n34\tstates rather than counts. Three things broke, and each was something contract 1\n35\thad got wrong rather than merely lacked.\n36\t\n37\t| What contract 1 did | Why nobody noticed | What contract 2 does |\n38\t|---|---|---|\n39\t| journey evidence was **numeric only** (`journey.count`) | a funnel is countable in every part that matters — three leads, one won | `journey.fact` observes a **stated outcome**: `at_risk`, `breached`, `false`. `slaEvaluations: 2` is equally true of a run that recorded the wrong answer twice |\n40\t| the report never said **which clock** produced the evidence | nothing in a funnel is a function of the current instant | `journey.clock` is published from the frozen registry. An SLA state is a function of the clock and of nothing else |\n41\t| `limitations[]` was a **single global list** | with one journey, every limitation was true of every run | limitations carry a `scope`, and a journey declares its own. \"No business-hours calendar\" is meaningless for a lead funnel; \"no external enrichment provider\" is meaningless for a support case |\n42\t\n43\tFour things did **not** change, and the second consumer is the reason we can say\n44\tso rather than assume it:\n45\t\n46\t- **the document contract stayed at `scenarioContract: 1`.** A vocabulary gained\n47\t  an entry; nothing existing changed meaning, and every v1 scenario still\n48\t  validates. The **report** contract moved to `2`, because the report gained\n49\t  fields and every fingerprint moved with them — a consumer diffing fingerprints\n50\t  across that boundary deserves to be told rather than to discover it.\n51\t- **no `requires` block.** Service needs the contracts package and the\n52\t  capability `contracts/service-obligations@1`, and it states that as\n53\t  `package.composed` and `capability.available` *observations that must pass* —\n54\t  answered by AX1, failing closed. A prerequisites block would be a second way\n55\t  to say the same thing, and worse: a precondition invites \"skip if unmet\",\n56\t  which turns a failure into silence.\n57\t- **no record-graph query.** \"The escalation cites the SLA evaluation it was\n58\t  raised on\" is a link between two records, and the contract does not learn to\n59\t  traverse them. The journey — trusted, checked-in source — asserts the link and\n60\t  publishes it as one fact. A query language over records is exactly the slope\n61\t  that ends in an executable format.\n62\t- **no negation operator.** \"Nobody was notified\" is published as\n63\t  `escalationNotified = false` and observed as `false`, not as an assertion that\n64\t  something is absent. A run can earn an absence by never attempting the\n65\t  operation; the service journey attempts every refusal it reports.\n66\t\n67\t## What it is not\n68\t\n69\t**It is not a second test runner.** It runs no suite, grades no package and\n70\tcalls no doctor — that is `accordo project verify`, and duplicating it would leave\n71\ttwo commands answering almost the same question. What it produces is the mapping\n72\tprose cannot give you: *this scenario ran, against this composition, these rows\n73\tit exercised with linked evidence, and these rows it did **not** establish.*\n74\t\n75\t**It promotes nothing.** `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §3 gives the four-value JTBD\n76\tstatus vocabulary and puts the burden of proof on the higher value, decided by a\n77\tperson reviewing merged tests. This command reports evidence. Its own per-claim\n78\tvocabulary — `established | not_established | unresolved` — is deliberately not\n79\tthe JTBD vocabulary, so the two can never be read as the same thing, and every\n80\treport carries a `promotion` block recording that it performed none. `jobs.json`\n81\tand the matrix are opened read-only.\n82\t\n83\t**The negative is the point.** A scenario that earns fifteen rows and cannot\n84\tspeak to a hundred and thirty-four has to say so in its own output, so\n85\t`jtbd.notEstablished` is a counted, sectioned, fully enumerated list. \"Not\n86\testablished\" means *this scenario said nothing about the row* — never that the\n87\trow is unsupported. The recorded status in the index is the only statement about\n88\tsupport.\n89\t\n90\t## The scenario document\n91\t\n92\tA scenario is a **checked-in declarative document**, not a script:\n93\t`examples/scenarios/<id>.scenario.json`. A bare id on the command line resolves\n94\tthere; anything with a path separator is treated as an explicit path.\n95\t\n96\t```json\n97\t{\n98\t  \"scenarioContract\": 1,\n99\t  \"id\": \"lead-to-won\",\n100\t  \"title\": \"Lead to won, and everything the won deal sets in motion\",\n101\t  \"summary\": \"…\",\n102\t  \"journey\": \"b2b-lead-qualification\",\n103\t  \"steps\": [\n104\t    {\n105\t      \"id\": \"capture\",\n106\t      \"narrative\": \"Leads are captured into the starter's Lead record.\",\n107\t      \"observe\": [\n108\t        { \"kind\": \"module.present\", \"module\": \"lead\" },\n109\t        { \"kind\": \"journey.count\", \"metric\": \"leads\", \"atLeast\": 3 }\n110\t      ]\n111\t    }\n112\t  ],\n113\t  \"claims\": [\n114\t    { \"job\": \"JTBD-04\", \"steps\": [\"capture\"], \"note\": \"narrow wording…\" }\n115\t  ]\n116\t}\n117\t```\n118\t\n119\t- **`journey`** names, by id, the thing the runner executes. Ids resolve against\n120\t  a frozen registry in the runner's own source — today two entries, both\n121\t  checked-in repository source that CI runs. A journey is a *stronger* authority\n122\t  than DX6, never a weaker one: its own in-process assertions are the claim, and\n123\t  DX6 adds the mapping nobody has. The registry, not the document, also declares\n124\t  the journey's **clock** and the journey's **own limitations**.\n125\t- **`steps`** are the business narrative, in order. Each declares observations.\n126\t- **`claims`** map JTBD ids, resolved against `jobs.json`, to the steps that are\n127\t  their evidence. A claim is `established` only when *every* observation in\n128\t  *every* cited step passed.\n129\t\n130\t### How it refuses to carry a command\n131\t\n132\t`docs/CODER_TOOLING_ROADMAP.md` refuses \"executable commands as trusted plan\n133\tcontent\" — *a format that can describe execution invites a runtime, and the\n134\tfirst runtime that reads a plan is one edit away from applying it* — and the\n135\tSolution Plan validator enforces that rather than documenting it. A scenario\n136\trefuses the same thing, in three independent layers:\n137\t\n138\t1. **Shape.** No field in the contract could hold a command. Every object is\n139\t   checked against a closed key allow-list, so `{\"run\": \"npm test\"}` is refused\n140\t   as `SCENARIO_FIELD_UNKNOWN` *before its value is read*.\n141\t2. **Content.** Every string at every depth goes through `EXECUTABLE_SHAPES` —\n142\t   **the same exported constant the Solution Plan validator uses**, not a second\n143\t   copy that gets fixed once. A shell command, a command substitution, shell\n144\t   chaining, a remote address or a script tag is `SCENARIO_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`.\n145\t3. **Path.** There is no code path from a document value to an invocation. The\n146\t   only thing the runner spawns is a journey selected by id from that frozen\n147\t   registry. A scenario cannot name a path, a script, an argument, an\n148\t   interpreter or an environment variable. The single field that names a file —\n149\t   a Solution Plan citation — is read and validated as a document, never\n150\t   executed, and is refused if it is absolute, contains `..`, uses a backslash\n151\t   or a URL scheme, or resolves outside the project. \"Outside the project\" is\n152\t   decided on the **canonical** path, not the written one: a lexical check\n153\t   cannot see a symlink, and `docs/plans/x.json` can be a well-formed relative\n154\t   path that reads somewhere else entirely. ADR-026 settled this for the\n155\t   publication assembler; the same rule holds here. A link that stays inside the\n156\t   project is still followed — the boundary is where the bytes are, not whether\n157\t   a link was used to reach them.\n158\t\n159\t**A document with any problem never starts a journey.** The refusal costs\n160\tnothing and happens first.\n161\t\n162\t### The observation vocabulary\n163\t\n164\tClosed, and owned by the runner rather than the document — the same discipline\n165\t`accordo project verify` uses for declared scripts, where the project declares a\n166\t*name* and the framework owns the list.\n167\t\n168\t| Kind | Authority | Answers |\n169\t|---|---|---|\n170\t| `journey.completed` | journey | the journey ran to completion and reported success |\n171\t| `journey.count` | journey | a numeric metric the journey reported (`atLeast` or `equals`, exactly one) |\n172\t| `journey.fact` | journey | a **stated outcome** the journey reported (`fact` and `is`), compared exactly |\n173\t| `package.composed` | `app inspect` | the composed application includes this domain package |\n174\t| `resource.present` | `app inspect` | a domain package in this composition owns this resource |\n175\t| `module.present` | `app inspect` | the composition has this record module (a project's own, or package-owned) |\n176\t| `action.present` | `app inspect` | the composition publishes this `module.action` |\n177\t| `capability.available` | `app inspect` | a declared cross-package capability **resolved** here (declared-but-unresolved fails) |\n178\t| `policy.present` | `app inspect` | a versioned policy this composition registered |\n179\t| `plan.valid` | Solution Plan | a checked-in plan this scenario is the evidence run for, read as a document |\n180\t\n181\tComposition facts are taken **verbatim** from the AX1 report. Nothing an\n182\tupstream authority already resolved is recomputed here.\n183\t\n184\t**Which authority answers what** is the line the second consumer made explicit.\n185\t`app inspect` answers *what this application is* — read from checked-in source,\n186\topening no database and contacting no provider. The journey answers *what\n187\thappened when it ran* — the only channel for a runtime fact, because AX1 by\n188\tconstruction cannot see one. `journey.count` and `journey.fact` are that channel:\n189\tcounts for how many, facts for which. Audit, event and trace facts belong there\n190\ttoo, as counts the journey publishes about the application it drove\n191\t(`auditEvents`, `workflowRuns`); the contract does **not** grow an authority that\n192\topens the journey's database, because a second reader of runtime state is a\n193\tsecond thing to keep honest.\n194\t\n195\t### `journey.fact`, and the rule that keeps prose out\n196\t\n197\tA fact is a **boolean**, or a string that is one lower-case token of at most 64\n198\tcharacters (`/^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$/`). Booleans are published as `true` /\n199\t`false`, so one closed grammar covers every fact and a document never carries a\n200\tJSON type. A summary sentence has spaces, capitals and length, so it is excluded\n201\t*by construction* rather than by a denylist somebody has to maintain — and a\n202\tshell command, a URL, a path and a command substitution are all refused twice,\n203\tonce by that grammar and once by `EXECUTABLE_SHAPES`.\n204\t\n205\t```json\n206\t{ \"kind\": \"journey.fact\", \"fact\": \"firstResponseStateAtDueInstant\", \"is\": \"at_risk\" }\n207\t{ \"kind\": \"journey.fact\", \"fact\": \"escalationNotified\", \"is\": \"false\" }\n208\t```\n209\t\n210\t## The report\n211\t\n212\t`scenarioRunContract: 2`, canonically ordered, with `scenario`, `status`,\n213\t`counts`, `journey`, `composition`, `steps`, `observations`, `jtbd`,\n214\t`promotion`, `problems`, `limitations` and a semantic `fingerprint`.\n215\t\n216\t`journey` carries `clock` (`wall-clock` or `injected-fixed`, from the registry),\n217\t`metrics` and `facts`. `limitations` entries carry `scope`: `global` for what is\n218\ttrue of every run, `journey` for what the journey that ran declares about\n219\titself. A journey may **add** to what a run does not prove; it may never\n220\tsubtract, and a scenario document may do neither — there is no field in the\n221\tshape that could hold a limitation.\n222\t\n223\t```text\n224\texit 0   the scenario ran and every claim is established\n225\texit 1   the scenario ran and something did not hold, or the document is\n226\t         invalid — in which case no journey was started\n227\texit 2   the scenario document could not be read at all\n228\t```\n229\t\n230\t**Deterministic and offline.** No duration, timestamp, temporary path, machine\n231\tlayout or random value enters the report *at all* — not merely the fingerprint —\n232\tso two runs of an unchanged checkout produce byte-identical documents from\n233\tdifferent working directories. Nothing reaches the network. Three fingerprints\n234\ttravel with every report: the scenario document's own, the composition's\n235\t(`inspectionFingerprint`, shared with AX2), and the JTBD index's.\n236\t\n237\tThe journey composes a whole application in a temporary directory outside the\n238\trepository, which is removed afterwards. **Nothing is written into the project\n239\tbeing reported on**, which is why there is no worktree check to get wrong.\n240\t\n241\t### Problem codes\n242\t\n243\t`SCENARIO_UNREADABLE`, `SCENARIO_TOO_LARGE`, `SCENARIO_CONTRACT_UNSUPPORTED`,\n244\t`SCENARIO_FIELD_INVALID`, `SCENARIO_FIELD_UNKNOWN`,\n245\t`SCENARIO_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`, `SCENARIO_VOCABULARY_UNKNOWN`,\n246\t`SCENARIO_DUPLICATE_ID`, `SCENARIO_CITATION_UNRESOLVED`, `SCENARIO_PATH_REFUSED`,\n247\t`SCENARIO_JOURNEY_UNKNOWN`, `SCENARIO_JOURNEY_FAILED`, `SCENARIO_JOB_UNKNOWN`,\n248\t`SCENARIO_JOBS_INDEX_UNREADABLE`, `SCENARIO_COMPOSITION_UNREADABLE`,\n249\t`SCENARIO_RECURSION_REFUSED`.\n250\t\n251\tEverything fails closed. A claim whose job id is not in the index, a step id that\n252\tdoes not exist, a metric the journey never reported, a capability that is\n253\tdeclared but unresolved — each fails the run rather than passing by having\n254\tnothing to check.\n255\t\n256\t### What it does not prove — global, published on every run\n257\t\n258\t| Code | Means |\n259\t|---|---|\n260\t| `SCENARIO_IS_NOT_PROMOTION` | this changes no JTBD status and writes to no document |\n261\t| `COVERAGE_IS_CLAIMED_NOT_DISCOVERED` | it checks the rows a scenario *claims*; it cannot discover which rows a journey happens to exercise |\n262\t| `NEGATIVE_EVIDENCE_IS_SILENCE` | a row under `notEstablished` means the scenario said nothing about it, never that it is unsupported |\n263\t| `EVIDENCE_IS_ONE_COMPOSITION` | the evidence is about the one application this journey composed |\n264\t| `JOURNEY_SOURCE_TRUSTED` | the journey is checked-in repository source running with the operator's authority. The child is bounded in time, output and process group — isolation, not a sandbox |\n265\t| `BROWSER_EVIDENCE_NOT_AUTOMATED` | no browser is driven; nothing here is evidence about the Admin as a user sees it |\n266\t| `NO_PROVIDER_CONTACTED` | offline by construction; every provider in the journey is a checked-in fixture |\n267\t| `PRODUCTION_READINESS_NOT_ASSESSED` | no auth, tenancy or RBAC exists, and nothing here assesses deployment or operational readiness |\n268\t\n269\t### What the journey does not prove — journey-scoped\n270\t\n271\t| Journey | Codes |\n272\t|---|---|\n273\t| `b2b-lead-qualification` | `JOURNEY_CLOCK_IS_WALL_CLOCK`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVIDER_IS_A_FIXTURE` |\n274\t| `service-sla-escalation` | `SLA_IS_ELAPSED_TIME_NOT_A_CONTRACTUAL_JUDGEMENT`, `NOTHING_WAS_NOTIFIED_OR_ROUTED`, `ESCALATION_IS_MANUALLY_RECORDED`, `SERVICE_COVERAGE_IS_NOT_A_CONTRACT` |\n275\t\n276\tNo code appears in more than one list — not in both journeys, and not shadowing a\n277\tglobal one. A limitation true of both journeys belongs in the global set; a\n278\tlimitation true of neither is noise that teaches a reader to skip the ones that\n279\tmatter; and one code with two messages is a report that contradicts itself. All\n280\tthree are test-pinned, as is the rule that every registered journey declares a\n281\tclock and at least one limitation of its own.\n282\t\n283\t## Two worked runs\n284\t\n285\t```console\n286\t$ npm run crm -- scenario run lead-to-won --json\n287\t```\n288\t\n289\tThe first scenario runs the B2B starter journey on the **wall clock** and claims\n290\tfifteen rows across capture, qualification, conversion, pipeline, enrichment,\n291\tscoring, routing, quoting, discount approval, signature and order, contract\n292\tactivation, delivery handover, custom-package authoring, cross-package capability\n293\tand application discovery. It reports **fifteen established** and **one hundred\n294\tand thirty-four not established**, sectioned, over a composition of six packages.\n295\t\n296\tOne of the fifteen — `JTBD-CS-01`, contract activation — is recorded as\n297\t*partially supported*, and stays that way. The run shows the activation\n298\thappening; the row's missing part (a pre-signature term snapshot) is not\n299\tsomething a run can supply, and only a person may move a status.\n300\t\n301\t```console\n302\t$ npm run crm -- scenario run service-sla-escalation --json\n303\t```\n304\t\n305\tThe second runs the service journey on an **injected, stepped clock**, over a\n306\tcomposition of **two** packages — `contracts` and `service`, and nothing else,\n307\twhich is what \"Service reaches contracts only through a declared capability\"\n308\tlooks like when it is demonstrated instead of asserted. It claims five rows and\n309\treports **five established** and **one hundred and forty-four not established**.\n310\t\n311\tIts centre is three instants:\n312\t\n313\t```text\n314\topenedAt                     2026-09-15T11:00:00.000Z\n315\tfirstResponseDueAt           2026-09-15T15:00:00.000Z   openedAt + 240 minutes\n316\t  evaluated at the due instant        firstResponseState = at_risk\n317\t  evaluated one millisecond later     firstResponseState = breached\n318\t```\n319\t\n320\tThat boundary is the reason the journey injects a clock. `evaluateSla()` reads\n321\t`now()` and exposes no `at` parameter, so on the wall clock the boundary is four\n322\thours away and no run can ever reach it. A regression turning `now > due` into\n323\t`now >= due` changes a business outcome and nothing else, and this is the only\n324\trun in the repository that fails on it.\n325\t\n326\tBoth service rows it claims — `JTBD-DS-11` and `JTBD-DS-12` — are recorded as\n327\t*partially supported* and stay that way. What the run shows is real; what it does\n328\tnot show is published in the journey's own limitations, including that **nothing\n329\twas notified, routed or billed** — each of those a fact the journey states as\n330\t`false` and the scenario observes as `false`, because a refusal a run could earn\n331\tby never attempting the operation is worth nothing.\n332\t\n333\t## Adding a scenario\n334\t\n335\tWrite `examples/scenarios/<id>.scenario.json`, claim only rows the journey\n336\tgenuinely exercises, and run it. No code changes. A *new journey* does need a\n337\tregistry entry in `packages/cli/src/scenario-journey.js`, deliberately: that is\n338\tthe boundary that keeps a document from naming something to run. A new journey\n339\talso declares its **clock** and its **own limitations** there, for the same\n340\treason — a document that could choose the instant could choose the one where the\n341\tbreach disappears, and a document that could write its own limitations could\n342\twrite a shorter list.\n343\t\n344\t## Relationship to `accordo project verify` (DX5)\n345\t\n346\t`accordo project verify` **does not run scenarios**, and that was re-examined when\n347\tthe second one landed. It stays a separate, explicit command, and DX5 keeps\n348\tpublishing `SCENARIO_EVIDENCE_NOT_RUN`.\n349\t\n350\tThe alternative considered was a bounded applicability contract — scenarios\n351\tdeclaring themselves `required` or `current`, the way Solution Plans do, so DX5\n352\tcould run the declared ones. It is refused for now:\n353\t\n354\t- each scenario composes a **whole application** of its own, so the cost is\n355\t  minutes per scenario and grows with every one a project adds. Making the\n356\t  cheap-to-reason-about command silently multi-minute is the opposite of what\n357\t  it is for;\n358\t- a declaration vocabulary is a new agent-facing contract, and the DX Simplicity\n359\t  Gate asks which *failure mode* it prevents rather than which capability it\n360\t  adds;\n361\t- the failure mode it would prevent — \"somebody forgot to run the scenario\" — is\n362\t  already named in machine-readable form by `SCENARIO_EVIDENCE_NOT_RUN`, which a\n363\t  caller can switch on today.\n364\t\n365\tWhat would change the answer: a scenario registry with declared applicability\n366\tthat exists for reasons other than DX5. DX10 has since arrived, and it did **not**\n367\tchange the answer — it made the relationship explicit in the other direction.\n368\t`accordo solution verify` runs Project Verify and each **explicitly referenced**\n369\tscenario, once each, because an evidence document names exactly the scenarios its\n370\trequirements rest on. That is the applicability declaration the alternative\n371\twanted, and it lives where the *consumer* is declared rather than inside the\n372\tscenario, for the reason ADR-029 already gives.\n373\t\n374\t## Related\n375\t\n376\t`DECISIONS.md` ADR-029 (why a second consumer is validation work, and why a\n377\tconsumer-specific bound is declared where the consumer is) ·\n378\t`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` (§3, the status vocabulary and who may change it) ·\n379\t`docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` · `docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md` (AX1) ·\n380\t`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md` (AX2) · `docs/CODER_TOOLING_ROADMAP.md` ·\n381\t`docs/plans/dx6-scenario-runner.md` (the ExecPlan, with the eight DX Simplicity\n382\tGate answers) · `docs/plans/dx6-second-scenario.md` (the second consumer, and\n383\twhat it changed) · `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_EVIDENCE.md` (DX10, which cites a\n384\tscenario observation as requirement-level evidence) · `docs/SERVICE_OPERATIONS.md` (what Service does and,\n385\tcrucially, does not do) · `tests/scenario-document.test.js` ·\n386\t`tests/scenario-run.test.js` ·\n387\t`examples/journeys/service-sla-escalation/journey.mjs`.\n388\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"49d2d6e5-d6fb-4573-ba37-4afadca8dd89","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:34:55.652Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-07-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/docs/SCENARIO_EVIDENCE.md","content":"# Scenario evidence\n\n`accordo scenario run <scenario> [--json] [--root dir]` — **DX6**.\n\n> **Which JTBD rows does this checkout actually earn — and which does it not?**\n\nJob status in this repository lives in `docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` and\nits generated index `docs/benchmarks/jobs.json`. Both are maintained by people.\nNothing recomputed them, and nothing could tell you what a given run did **not**\nestablish — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` lists that as a known limitation, and AX1\nsays it in its own report: `evidence.status` is `not_aggregated`, with a note\nthat the JTBD documents are referenced by path and never parsed.\n\nThis command closes that gap, and only that gap.\n\n```text\naccordo app inspect      what is composed?                             source facts\naccordo solution check   is this plan still compatible?                a document\naccordo project doctor   what is stale in the source?                  cheap\naccordo project verify   can we PROVE the project is healthy?          expensive\naccordo scenario run     which business jobs does this checkout earn?  this\naccordo solution verify  is the plan actually finished?                DX10, which runs this\n```\n\n## Two consumers, and what the second one changed\n\nA generic contract validated by exactly one consumer is not generic. It is a\nshape fitted to that consumer, wearing a version number.\n\n`scenarioRunContract: 1` shipped with one scenario — `lead-to-won`, a sales\nfunnel. The second one, `service-sla-escalation`, was written specifically to be\n*unlike* it: Service is package-native, has a declared state machine, real clock\nsemantics and immutable evidence records, and its load-bearing outcomes are\nstates rather than counts. Three things broke, and each was something contract 1\nhad got wrong rather than merely lacked.\n\n| What contract 1 did | Why nobody noticed | What contract 2 does |\n|---|---|---|\n| journey evidence was **numeric only** (`journey.count`) | a funnel is countable in every part that matters — three leads, one won | `journey.fact` observes a **stated outcome**: `at_risk`, `breached`, `false`. `slaEvaluations: 2` is equally true of a run that recorded the wrong answer twice |\n| the report never said **which clock** produced the evidence | nothing in a funnel is a function of the current instant | `journey.clock` is published from the frozen registry. An SLA state is a function of the clock and of nothing else |\n| `limitations[]` was a **single global list** | with one journey, every limitation was true of every run | limitations carry a `scope`, and a journey declares its own. \"No business-hours calendar\" is meaningless for a lead funnel; \"no external enrichment provider\" is meaningless for a support case |\n\nFour things did **not** change, and the second consumer is the reason we can say\nso rather than assume it:\n\n- **the document contract stayed at `scenarioContract: 1`.** A vocabulary gained\n  an entry; nothing existing changed meaning, and every v1 scenario still\n  validates. The **report** contract moved to `2`, because the report gained\n  fields and every fingerprint moved with them — a consumer diffing fingerprints\n  across that boundary deserves to be told rather than to discover it.\n- **no `requires` block.** Service needs the contracts package and the\n  capability `contracts/service-obligations@1`, and it states that as\n  `package.composed` and `capability.available` *observations that must pass* —\n  answered by AX1, failing closed. A prerequisites block would be a second way\n  to say the same thing, and worse: a precondition invites \"skip if unmet\",\n  which turns a failure into silence.\n- **no record-graph query.** \"The escalation cites the SLA evaluation it was\n  raised on\" is a link between two records, and the contract does not learn to\n  traverse them. The journey — trusted, checked-in source — asserts the link and\n  publishes it as one fact. A query language over records is exactly the slope\n  that ends in an executable format.\n- **no negation operator.** \"Nobody was notified\" is published as\n  `escalationNotified = false` and observed as `false`, not as an assertion that\n  something is absent. A run can earn an absence by never attempting the\n  operation; the service journey attempts every refusal it reports.\n\n## What it is not\n\n**It is not a second test runner.** It runs no suite, grades no package and\ncalls no doctor — that is `accordo project verify`, and duplicating it would leave\ntwo commands answering almost the same question. What it produces is the mapping\nprose cannot give you: *this scenario ran, against this composition, these rows\nit exercised with linked evidence, and these rows it did **not** establish.*\n\n**It promotes nothing.** `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §3 gives the four-value JTBD\nstatus vocabulary and puts the burden of proof on the higher value, decided by a\nperson reviewing merged tests. This command reports evidence. Its own per-claim\nvocabulary — `established | not_established | unresolved` — is deliberately not\nthe JTBD vocabulary, so the two can never be read as the same thing, and every\nreport carries a `promotion` block recording that it performed none. `jobs.json`\nand the matrix are opened read-only.\n\n**The negative is the point.** A scenario that earns fifteen rows and cannot\nspeak to a hundred and thirty-four has to say so in its own output, so\n`jtbd.notEstablished` is a counted, sectioned, fully enumerated list. \"Not\nestablished\" means *this scenario said nothing about the row* — never that the\nrow is unsupported. The recorded status in the index is the only statement about\nsupport.\n\n## The scenario document\n\nA scenario is a **checked-in declarative document**, not a script:\n`examples/scenarios/<id>.scenario.json`. A bare id on the command line resolves\nthere; anything with a path separator is treated as an explicit path.\n\n```json\n{\n  \"scenarioContract\": 1,\n  \"id\": \"lead-to-won\",\n  \"title\": \"Lead to won, and everything the won deal sets in motion\",\n  \"summary\": \"…\",\n  \"journey\": \"b2b-lead-qualification\",\n  \"steps\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"capture\",\n      \"narrative\": \"Leads are captured into the starter's Lead record.\",\n      \"observe\": [\n        { \"kind\": \"module.present\", \"module\": \"lead\" },\n        { \"kind\": \"journey.count\", \"metric\": \"leads\", \"atLeast\": 3 }\n      ]\n    }\n  ],\n  \"claims\": [\n    { \"job\": \"JTBD-04\", \"steps\": [\"capture\"], \"note\": \"narrow wording…\" }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n- **`journey`** names, by id, the thing the runner executes. Ids resolve against\n  a frozen registry in the runner's own source — today two entries, both\n  checked-in repository source that CI runs. A journey is a *stronger* authority\n  than DX6, never a weaker one: its own in-process assertions are the claim, and\n  DX6 adds the mapping nobody has. The registry, not the document, also declares\n  the journey's **clock** and the journey's **own limitations**.\n- **`steps`** are the business narrative, in order. Each declares observations.\n- **`claims`** map JTBD ids, resolved against `jobs.json`, to the steps that are\n  their evidence. A claim is `established` only when *every* observation in\n  *every* cited step passed.\n\n### How it refuses to carry a command\n\n`docs/CODER_TOOLING_ROADMAP.md` refuses \"executable commands as trusted plan\ncontent\" — *a format that can describe execution invites a runtime, and the\nfirst runtime that reads a plan is one edit away from applying it* — and the\nSolution Plan validator enforces that rather than documenting it. A scenario\nrefuses the same thing, in three independent layers:\n\n1. **Shape.** No field in the contract could hold a command. Every object is\n   checked against a closed key allow-list, so `{\"run\": \"npm test\"}` is refused\n   as `SCENARIO_FIELD_UNKNOWN` *before its value is read*.\n2. **Content.** Every string at every depth goes through `EXECUTABLE_SHAPES` —\n   **the same exported constant the Solution Plan validator uses**, not a second\n   copy that gets fixed once. A shell command, a command substitution, shell\n   chaining, a remote address or a script tag is `SCENARIO_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`.\n3. **Path.** There is no code path from a document value to an invocation. The\n   only thing the runner spawns is a journey selected by id from that frozen\n   registry. A scenario cannot name a path, a script, an argument, an\n   interpreter or an environment variable. The single field that names a file —\n   a Solution Plan citation — is read and validated as a document, never\n   executed, and is refused if it is absolute, contains `..`, uses a backslash\n   or a URL scheme, or resolves outside the project. \"Outside the project\" is\n   decided on the **canonical** path, not the written one: a lexical check\n   cannot see a symlink, and `docs/plans/x.json` can be a well-formed relative\n   path that reads somewhere else entirely. ADR-026 settled this for the\n   publication assembler; the same rule holds here. A link that stays inside the\n   project is still followed — the boundary is where the bytes are, not whether\n   a link was used to reach them.\n\n**A document with any problem never starts a journey.** The refusal costs\nnothing and happens first.\n\n### The observation vocabulary\n\nClosed, and owned by the runner rather than the document — the same discipline\n`accordo project verify` uses for declared scripts, where the project declares a\n*name* and the framework owns the list.\n\n| Kind | Authority | Answers |\n|---|---|---|\n| `journey.completed` | journey | the journey ran to completion and reported success |\n| `journey.count` | journey | a numeric metric the journey reported (`atLeast` or `equals`, exactly one) |\n| `journey.fact` | journey | a **stated outcome** the journey reported (`fact` and `is`), compared exactly |\n| `package.composed` | `app inspect` | the composed application includes this domain package |\n| `resource.present` | `app inspect` | a domain package in this composition owns this resource |\n| `module.present` | `app inspect` | the composition has this record module (a project's own, or package-owned) |\n| `action.present` | `app inspect` | the composition publishes this `module.action` |\n| `capability.available` | `app inspect` | a declared cross-package capability **resolved** here (declared-but-unresolved fails) |\n| `policy.present` | `app inspect` | a versioned policy this composition registered |\n| `plan.valid` | Solution Plan | a checked-in plan this scenario is the evidence run for, read as a document |\n\nComposition facts are taken **verbatim** from the AX1 report. Nothing an\nupstream authority already resolved is recomputed here.\n\n**Which authority answers what** is the line the second consumer made explicit.\n`app inspect` answers *what this application is* — read from checked-in source,\nopening no database and contacting no provider. The journey answers *what\nhappened when it ran* — the only channel for a runtime fact, because AX1 by\nconstruction cannot see one. `journey.count` and `journey.fact` are that channel:\ncounts for how many, facts for which. Audit, event and trace facts belong there\ntoo, as counts the journey publishes about the application it drove\n(`auditEvents`, `workflowRuns`); the contract does **not** grow an authority that\nopens the journey's database, because a second reader of runtime state is a\nsecond thing to keep honest.\n\n### `journey.fact`, and the rule that keeps prose out\n\nA fact is a **boolean**, or a string that is one lower-case token of at most 64\ncharacters (`/^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$/`). Booleans are published as `true` /\n`false`, so one closed grammar covers every fact and a document never carries a\nJSON type. A summary sentence has spaces, capitals and length, so it is excluded\n*by construction* rather than by a denylist somebody has to maintain — and a\nshell command, a URL, a path and a command substitution are all refused twice,\nonce by that grammar and once by `EXECUTABLE_SHAPES`.\n\n```json\n{ \"kind\": \"journey.fact\", \"fact\": \"firstResponseStateAtDueInstant\", \"is\": \"at_risk\" }\n{ \"kind\": \"journey.fact\", \"fact\": \"escalationNotified\", \"is\": \"false\" }\n```\n\n## The report\n\n`scenarioRunContract: 2`, canonically ordered, with `scenario`, `status`,\n`counts`, `journey`, `composition`, `steps`, `observations`, `jtbd`,\n`promotion`, `problems`, `limitations` and a semantic `fingerprint`.\n\n`journey` carries `clock` (`wall-clock` or `injected-fixed`, from the registry),\n`metrics` and `facts`. `limitations` entries carry `scope`: `global` for what is\ntrue of every run, `journey` for what the journey that ran declares about\nitself. A journey may **add** to what a run does not prove; it may never\nsubtract, and a scenario document may do neither — there is no field in the\nshape that could hold a limitation.\n\n```text\nexit 0   the scenario ran and every claim is established\nexit 1   the scenario ran and something did not hold, or the document is\n         invalid — in which case no journey was started\nexit 2   the scenario document could not be read at all\n```\n\n**Deterministic and offline.** No duration, timestamp, temporary path, machine\nlayout or random value enters the report *at all* — not merely the fingerprint —\nso two runs of an unchanged checkout produce byte-identical documents from\ndifferent working directories. Nothing reaches the network. Three fingerprints\ntravel with every report: the scenario document's own, the composition's\n(`inspectionFingerprint`, shared with AX2), and the JTBD index's.\n\nThe journey composes a whole application in a temporary directory outside the\nrepository, which is removed afterwards. **Nothing is written into the project\nbeing reported on**, which is why there is no worktree check to get wrong.\n\n### Problem codes\n\n`SCENARIO_UNREADABLE`, `SCENARIO_TOO_LARGE`, `SCENARIO_CONTRACT_UNSUPPORTED`,\n`SCENARIO_FIELD_INVALID`, `SCENARIO_FIELD_UNKNOWN`,\n`SCENARIO_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`, `SCENARIO_VOCABULARY_UNKNOWN`,\n`SCENARIO_DUPLICATE_ID`, `SCENARIO_CITATION_UNRESOLVED`, `SCENARIO_PATH_REFUSED`,\n`SCENARIO_JOURNEY_UNKNOWN`, `SCENARIO_JOURNEY_FAILED`, `SCENARIO_JOB_UNKNOWN`,\n`SCENARIO_JOBS_INDEX_UNREADABLE`, `SCENARIO_COMPOSITION_UNREADABLE`,\n`SCENARIO_RECURSION_REFUSED`.\n\nEverything fails closed. A claim whose job id is not in the index, a step id that\ndoes not exist, a metric the journey never reported, a capability that is\ndeclared but unresolved — each fails the run rather than passing by having\nnothing to check.\n\n### What it does not prove — global, published on every run\n\n| Code | Means |\n|---|---|\n| `SCENARIO_IS_NOT_PROMOTION` | this changes no JTBD status and writes to no document |\n| `COVERAGE_IS_CLAIMED_NOT_DISCOVERED` | it checks the rows a scenario *claims*; it cannot discover which rows a journey happens to exercise |\n| `NEGATIVE_EVIDENCE_IS_SILENCE` | a row under `notEstablished` means the scenario said nothing about it, never that it is unsupported |\n| `EVIDENCE_IS_ONE_COMPOSITION` | the evidence is about the one application this journey composed |\n| `JOURNEY_SOURCE_TRUSTED` | the journey is checked-in repository source running with the operator's authority. The child is bounded in time, output and process group — isolation, not a sandbox |\n| `BROWSER_EVIDENCE_NOT_AUTOMATED` | no browser is driven; nothing here is evidence about the Admin as a user sees it |\n| `NO_PROVIDER_CONTACTED` | offline by construction; every provider in the journey is a checked-in fixture |\n| `PRODUCTION_READINESS_NOT_ASSESSED` | no auth, tenancy or RBAC exists, and nothing here assesses deployment or operational readiness |\n\n### What the journey does not prove — journey-scoped\n\n| Journey | Codes |\n|---|---|\n| `b2b-lead-qualification` | `JOURNEY_CLOCK_IS_WALL_CLOCK`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVIDER_IS_A_FIXTURE` |\n| `service-sla-escalation` | `SLA_IS_ELAPSED_TIME_NOT_A_CONTRACTUAL_JUDGEMENT`, `NOTHING_WAS_NOTIFIED_OR_ROUTED`, `ESCALATION_IS_MANUALLY_RECORDED`, `SERVICE_COVERAGE_IS_NOT_A_CONTRACT` |\n\nNo code appears in more than one list — not in both journeys, and not shadowing a\nglobal one. A limitation true of both journeys belongs in the global set; a\nlimitation true of neither is noise that teaches a reader to skip the ones that\nmatter; and one code with two messages is a report that contradicts itself. All\nthree are test-pinned, as is the rule that every registered journey declares a\nclock and at least one limitation of its own.\n\n## Two worked runs\n\n```console\n$ npm run crm -- scenario run lead-to-won --json\n```\n\nThe first scenario runs the B2B starter journey on the **wall clock** and claims\nfifteen rows across capture, qualification, conversion, pipeline, enrichment,\nscoring, routing, quoting, discount approval, signature and order, contract\nactivation, delivery handover, custom-package authoring, cross-package capability\nand application discovery. It reports **fifteen established** and **one hundred\nand thirty-four not established**, sectioned, over a composition of six packages.\n\nOne of the fifteen — `JTBD-CS-01`, contract activation — is recorded as\n*partially supported*, and stays that way. The run shows the activation\nhappening; the row's missing part (a pre-signature term snapshot) is not\nsomething a run can supply, and only a person may move a status.\n\n```console\n$ npm run crm -- scenario run service-sla-escalation --json\n```\n\nThe second runs the service journey on an **injected, stepped clock**, over a\ncomposition of **two** packages — `contracts` and `service`, and nothing else,\nwhich is what \"Service reaches contracts only through a declared capability\"\nlooks like when it is demonstrated instead of asserted. It claims five rows and\nreports **five established** and **one hundred and forty-four not established**.\n\nIts centre is three instants:\n\n```text\nopenedAt                     2026-09-15T11:00:00.000Z\nfirstResponseDueAt           2026-09-15T15:00:00.000Z   openedAt + 240 minutes\n  evaluated at the due instant        firstResponseState = at_risk\n  evaluated one millisecond later     firstResponseState = breached\n```\n\nThat boundary is the reason the journey injects a clock. `evaluateSla()` reads\n`now()` and exposes no `at` parameter, so on the wall clock the boundary is four\nhours away and no run can ever reach it. A regression turning `now > due` into\n`now >= due` changes a business outcome and nothing else, and this is the only\nrun in the repository that fails on it.\n\nBoth service rows it claims — `JTBD-DS-11` and `JTBD-DS-12` — are recorded as\n*partially supported* and stay that way. What the run shows is real; what it does\nnot show is published in the journey's own limitations, including that **nothing\nwas notified, routed or billed** — each of those a fact the journey states as\n`false` and the scenario observes as `false`, because a refusal a run could earn\nby never attempting the operation is worth nothing.\n\n## Adding a scenario\n\nWrite `examples/scenarios/<id>.scenario.json`, claim only rows the journey\ngenuinely exercises, and run it. No code changes. A *new journey* does need a\nregistry entry in `packages/cli/src/scenario-journey.js`, deliberately: that is\nthe boundary that keeps a document from naming something to run. A new journey\nalso declares its **clock** and its **own limitations** there, for the same\nreason — a document that could choose the instant could choose the one where the\nbreach disappears, and a document that could write its own limitations could\nwrite a shorter list.\n\n## Relationship to `accordo project verify` (DX5)\n\n`accordo project verify` **does not run scenarios**, and that was re-examined when\nthe second one landed. It stays a separate, explicit command, and DX5 keeps\npublishing `SCENARIO_EVIDENCE_NOT_RUN`.\n\nThe alternative considered was a bounded applicability contract — scenarios\ndeclaring themselves `required` or `current`, the way Solution Plans do, so DX5\ncould run the declared ones. It is refused for now:\n\n- each scenario composes a **whole application** of its own, so the cost is\n  minutes per scenario and grows with every one a project adds. Making the\n  cheap-to-reason-about command silently multi-minute is the opposite of what\n  it is for;\n- a declaration vocabulary is a new agent-facing contract, and the DX Simplicity\n  Gate asks which *failure mode* it prevents rather than which capability it\n  adds;\n- the failure mode it would prevent — \"somebody forgot to run the scenario\" — is\n  already named in machine-readable form by `SCENARIO_EVIDENCE_NOT_RUN`, which a\n  caller can switch on today.\n\nWhat would change the answer: a scenario registry with declared applicability\nthat exists for reasons other than DX5. DX10 has since arrived, and it did **not**\nchange the answer — it made the relationship explicit in the other direction.\n`accordo solution verify` runs Project Verify and each **explicitly referenced**\nscenario, once each, because an evidence document names exactly the scenarios its\nrequirements rest on. That is the applicability declaration the alternative\nwanted, and it lives where the *consumer* is declared rather than inside the\nscenario, for the reason ADR-029 already gives.\n\n## Related\n\n`DECISIONS.md` ADR-029 (why a second consumer is validation work, and why a\nconsumer-specific bound is declared where the consumer is) ·\n`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` (§3, the status vocabulary and who may change it) ·\n`docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` · `docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md` (AX1) ·\n`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md` (AX2) · `docs/CODER_TOOLING_ROADMAP.md` ·\n`docs/plans/dx6-scenario-runner.md` (the ExecPlan, with the eight DX Simplicity\nGate answers) · `docs/plans/dx6-second-scenario.md` (the second consumer, and\nwhat it changed) · `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_EVIDENCE.md` (DX10, which cites a\nscenario observation as requirement-level evidence) · `docs/SERVICE_OPERATIONS.md` (what Service does and,\ncrucially, does not do) · `tests/scenario-document.test.js` ·\n`tests/scenario-run.test.js` ·\n`examples/journeys/service-sla-escalation/journey.mjs`.\n","numLines":388,"startLine":1,"totalLines":388}}}
